People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me today. Um, I hope I'm explaining this right, but I guess I'm here because... well, I've just been in a bit of a slump lately. I really don't want to sound lazy or anything, but I just don't seem to care about much anymore. 

I used to do a lot—I had my art classes, and I'd go to board-game nights with people. I loved hiking, too, and I was supposed to be getting back to my soccer team. But lately, I just... I don't know, I can't bring myself to care about any of it. I feel awful admitting it, because I know I should be out there doing things, but I just end up sitting in my room while everything sort of slips away from me. 

I'm sorry, I know I should be trying harder to keep up with my life. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.

Um, well... I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really don't want to sound like a broken record or like I'm not trying. I know I should be finding things to enjoy. But... no, not really.

Um... well, I really don't want to make a big deal out of it. I know you're probably asking because you want to make sure I'm taking care of myself, and I promise I'm trying to be good about that. I really am. 

But... I guess I have lost a little bit of weight. It's just... I go most of the day without eating lately. I try, but food just tastes like cardboard to me right now. I didn't even really notice the weight loss at first, but my jeans have gotten so loose that I actually have to knot the waistband to keep them up.

Um... well, I hope I'm explaining this right. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or anything, because I know everyone gets tired sometimes. I'm really trying to push through it. 

But... yeah, I guess there have been some changes. Sometimes I feel so heavy, like... moving just takes so much effort. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... well, I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I know I should be looking on the bright side, and I really try to be positive, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm not trying.

But... um, yeah. I guess it's been going on for a while now, definitely more than two weeks. It's just... most days, I wake up and I just have this really heavy, hollow feeling right here in my chest. It's like... like there's just no future for me outside of my apartment walls. 

I'm sorry, I know that sounds so dramatic and negative. I really don't mean to be a downer, and I'm trying to snap out of it, I really am. I just... I can't seem to shake that feeling lately. Is it okay that I feel that way? I hope I'm answering your question right.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I really wish I could say yes. I know I should have hobbies and things that keep me busy. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or being lazy, because I know how important it is to stay active. 

But... honestly, no. I just... I don't care about anything anymore. I used to love my art classes, and I'd go to board-game nights with friends... I even liked hiking and I was hoping to get back on my recreational soccer team. I know I should still be doing those things, and I feel terrible that I'm letting everyone down by not showing up. 

Now I just... I just sit in my room. It feels like everything is just slipping away from me, and I can't even find the energy to care about it. I'm so sorry, I know that's not what you want to hear, and I know I need to try harder to find things I enjoy. I really am trying to be better about it. Is it bad that I've let all those things go?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... oh gosh. I really, really don't want you to think I'm crazy, or... or that I'm making things up for attention. I know how bad this is going to sound, and I've been trying so hard to just ignore it and be rational, I promise I have. 

But... um, yeah. Sometimes... I do hear something. It's... it's a woman's voice. She usually talks to me from the corner of my room. She tells me that... well, she says that I ruined everything, and that I should just stay inside. And the thing is, it's not just like a thought in my head. It sounds as clear as someone standing right next to my bed. 

I'm so sorry, I know that sounds really alarming, and I don't want to worry you or be a burden. I'm probably just letting my stress get the best of me, right? I really am trying to push it away and just be normal. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I hope I'm not making things too difficult by bringing it up.

I know how bad this is going to sound, and I've been trying so hard to just ignore it and be rational, I promise I have. \n\nBut... um, yeah. Sometimes... I do hear something. It's... it's a woman's voice. She usually talks to me from the corner of my room. She tells me that... well, she says that I ruined everything, and that I should just stay inside. And the thing is, it's not just like a thought in my head. It sounds as clear as someone standing right next to my bed. \n\nI'm so sorry, I know that sounds really alarming, and I don't want to worry you or be a burden. I'm probably just letting my stress get the best of me, right? I really am trying to push it away and just be normal. Is it... is it okay that I'm telling you this? I hope I'm not making things too difficult by bringing it up.\n\nDoctor: Do the voices you hear comment on your actions or thoughts, or do they tell you to do or not do specific things?\n\nPatient: Um... well, she doesn't really comment on every little thing I do, if that's what you mean. I know some people hear voices that narrate their whole day, and I'm really glad it's not like that for me. I don't want to make it sound worse than it is. \n\nIt's mostly just... like I said, she tells me I ruined everything. And she does tell me what to do, but it's really just that one thing. She tells me I should stay inside. That's usually what she says when she's talking from the corner of my room. \n\nI really try not to listen to her, though. I know she's not real, even if she sounds exactly like someone standing right next to my bed. I'm doing the right thing by trying to ignore it, aren't I?\n\n[Last Question]\nDo the voices you hear ever tell you to harm yourself or others?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 34 / not_in_workforce / some_college\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Pleasing patient.\nA pleasing patient may\n1) minimize, downplay, or describe in more favorable terms symptoms and behaviors that they believe could create a negative impression,\n2) initially withhold or delay disclosing sensitive clinical information due to shame or fear of being judged, and\n3) seek confirmation that their descriptions or answers are appropriate and demonstrate a tendency to seek the doctor's approval.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. Oh! No, no, definitely not. Um... I really want to make sure you know that. She never says anything violent like that. I would never want to hurt anyone, or... or myself. I promise. I know how important it is to be safe, and I'm really trying to be good about all of this.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I mean, I guess it is, yeah. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining or just giving up, because I'm not. I'm really trying my best to keep it together. But... I suppose it is interfering a lot right now. 

Like I was saying, I've completely stopped taking on any of my freelance art-education work. I just... I don't want to disappoint any of the kids or the programs if I can't do a good job. I'm just not doing a very good job of reliably eating, or showering, or... or even just making sure I'm staying safe on my own. I know I should be handling all of this better at my age, and I really want to get back to normal. It's okay that I'm letting her help me while things are so hard, right? I'm just trying to make sure I don't let things get even worse.

I really am trying to be cooperative... is it okay that I don't have anything I enjoy right now?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of harming yourself or wishing you were dead?\n\nPatient: Um... well... I know this is one of those questions where there's a right answer and a wrong answer, and I really don't want to worry you or make you think I'm, you know, a danger or anything. I really don't want to be a burden. \n\nBut... I guess I should be honest, right? Because I want you to be able to help me. Um... sometimes... I just keep having these thoughts that I don't want to wake up anymore. It's not that I want to do anything bad, I just... I get so tired. \n\nAnd, um, there was this one time recently... my friend came over and found me, and I guess I had been saying out loud that I couldn't keep living like this. I didn't even realize I was doing it, and I felt so terrible for scaring her. I really didn't mean to. \n\nI'm so sorry if that's upsetting to hear. I promise I'm trying to hold on. Is... is it okay that I told you that? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight recently?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really don't want to make a big deal out of it. I know you're probably asking because you want to make sure I'm taking care of myself, and I promise I'm trying to be good about that. I really am. \n\nBut... I guess I have lost a little bit of weight. It's just... I go most of the day without eating lately. I try, but food just tastes like cardboard to me right now. I didn't even really notice the weight loss at first, but my jeans have gotten so loose that I actually have to knot the waistband to keep them up. \n\nI'm sorry, I know I should be eating better. I don't want you to think I'm neglecting myself on purpose or trying to be difficult. I'll try to do better, I promise. Does that... does that answer your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your energy level or the way you move?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I hope I'm explaining this right. I don't want to sound like I'm complaining or anything, because I know everyone gets tired sometimes. I'm really trying to push through it. \n\nBut... yeah, I guess there have been some changes. Sometimes I feel so heavy, like... moving just takes so much effort. Even just trying to make a cup of coffee feels like I'm trying to lift wet cement. I move so slowly, and I know it probably looks awful, and I'm sorry if I'm not being as active as I should be.\n\nAnd then... other times, it's like the opposite? I get this weird energy, but not a good kind. I'll just pace back and forth, from the kitchen to the window, over and over until my legs actually ache. I try to stop, I really do, because I know it's probably weird to watch, but I just can't settle down. \n\nI hope that makes sense. Is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about what you mean by feeling heavy and moving slowly, is that something that happens all the time or does it come and go?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it definitely comes and goes. I really hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, because I know I need to be more consistent. I try so hard to be. \n\nBut, um... yeah, it's not all the time. Like I was saying, there are days where I just... I move so slowly, and my arms and legs feel so heavy, like that wet cement feeling when I'm just trying to make coffee. And I feel awful because I know I should be getting things done. \n\nAnd then... other days, it's that pacing thing. I'll just walk back and forth, kitchen to the window, kitchen to the window, until my legs literally ache. It's like I can't find a middle ground, and I'm really sorry if that sounds confusing. I'm trying to manage it better, I promise. Does that... does that help explain it a little better?\n\nDoctor: Have you had a period of at least two weeks where you felt depressed most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um... well, I really don't want to sound like I'm just sitting around feeling sorry for myself. I know I should be looking on the bright side, and I really try to be positive, I promise. I don't want you to think I'm not trying.\n\nBut... um, yeah. I guess it's been going on for a while now, definitely more than two weeks. It's just... most days, I wake up and I just have this really heavy, hollow feeling right here in my chest. It's like... like there's just no future for me outside of my apartment walls. \n\nI'm sorry, I know that sounds so dramatic and negative. I really don't mean to be a downer, and I'm trying to snap out of it, I really am. I just... I can't seem to shake that feeling lately. Is it okay that I feel that way? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Are there any small things that still bring you some joy or pleasure?\n\nPatient: Um... I really wish I could say yes. I know I should have hobbies and things that keep me busy. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or being lazy, because I know how important it is to stay active. \n\nBut... honestly, no. It feels like everything is just slipping away from me, and I can't even find the energy to care about it. I'm so sorry, I know that's not what you want to hear, and I know I need to try harder to find things I enjoy. I really am trying to be better about it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I really hate to admit this, because I know how bad it sounds. I promise I'm not trying to just be lazy, and I know I should be pushing myself harder. But... yes, it really does feel that way. 

Like, even after I’ve been in bed for half the day—and I know I shouldn't be doing that, I really am trying to get up earlier—but even with all that rest, just... taking a shower, or even answering one single text message from my friend... it honestly feels like I’ve already worked a full shift.
